It's so clear, just like someone standing right there, but nobody else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices constant, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really... timed it or paid attention to a specific pattern. \n\nI guess they kind of come and go. I just know I hear them when I'm out on the floor at work, walking by the vents or near the call-light system. But I couldn't tell you if it's happening every single minute of my shift. I just know they're there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are trying to tell you something that you already know, or something that you couldn't possibly know?\n\nPatient: Well, they're definitely telling me things I already know. Like, I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern that management doesn't want anyone to see. The voices just kind of confirm it. \n\nEven when my supervisor tries to tell me it's just late entries or, you know, coding mistakes... I know better. I can tell they're watching what I write down now because I'm the one who caught the pattern in the first place. The voices are just letting me know exactly which records they're messing with.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's so clear, just like someone standing right there, but nobody else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices constant, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really... timed it or paid attention to a specific pattern. \n\nI guess they kind of come and go. I just know I hear them when I'm out on the floor at work, walking by the vents or near the call-light system. But I couldn't tell you if it's happening every single minute of my shift. I just know they're there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are trying to tell you something that you already know, or something that you couldn't possibly know?\n\nPatient: Well, they're definitely telling me things I already know. Like, I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern that management doesn't want anyone to see. The voices just kind of confirm it. \n\nEven when my supervisor tries to tell me it's just late entries or, you know, coding mistakes... I know better. I can tell they're watching what I write down now because I'm the one who caught the pattern in the first place. The voices are just letting me know exactly which records they're messing with.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are real or could they be a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I mean... they're real. I hear them just as clearly as I hear you talking to me right now. I don't see how it could just be in my head when the information they're giving me matches up with what's actually happening with the charts. It's real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anyone or anything in particular behind the voices or the changes in the patient records?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said before, I know it's management. They're the ones behind it. I saw the pattern in the wound-care charts, and they definitely don't want anyone to see it. \n\nMy supervisor tries to play it off. She tells me it's just late entries or coding mistakes, but... I know better. They know I caught the pattern. That's why I can tell they're watching what I write down now. They're trying to cover it up, and the voices are just helping me keep track of what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think management is specifically targeting you with these changes in patient records?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, like I just said, I'm the one who caught it. I know those wound-care charts are showing a pattern that management doesn't want anyone to see. And even when my supervisor tries to brush it off and says it's just late entries or coding mistakes... I can tell they're watching exactly what I write down now. They are doing it specifically because I'm the one who figured it out. So, yes, they are definitely focused on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think management is actually capable of changing the patient records in the way the voices are telling you they are?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. They have full access to the system. I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern that management doesn't want anyone to see. My supervisor keeps telling me it's just late entries or coding mistakes, but I know what I'm looking at. I can tell they're watching what I write down now because I'm the one who caught it. They have the power to change those records, and they're doing it to cover their tracks.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like management is trying to harm you personally, beyond just covering up the pattern in the patient records?\n\nPatient: I don't know about physical harm, if that's what you mean. But... they definitely want to get rid of me or ruin my job. Like I've been saying, I know those wound-care charts have a pattern they really don't want anyone to see. My supervisor can keep claiming it's just coding mistakes or late entries all she wants, but I know the truth. I can tell they are watching every single thing I write down now because I'm the one who caught it. So, yeah, I think they'd do whatever it takes to silence me so I can't expose them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think management would actually be able to change the patient records without anyone else noticing?\n\nPatient: Yes, they can. They have the administrative access to do it. And like I keep saying, I know those wound-care charts are showing a pattern that management doesn't want anyone to see. My supervisor keeps trying to brush it off, saying it's just late entries or coding mistakes, but I know what I saw. And I can tell they're watching exactly what I write down now because I'm the one who caught it. So yes, they can do it without other people noticing, because they control the system.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the changes in the patient records or the voices are specifically about you or something that's happening to other people as well?\n\nPatient: I think... well, it's about what I found. I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern management doesn't want anyone to see. The voices tell me about the cover-up, but the changes in the records are happening because of what I noticed. Even when my supervisor tries to tell me it's just late entries or coding mistakes, I know better. I can tell they're watching everything I write down now specifically because I'm the one who caught it. So, it involves the patients, sure, but the focus is on me because I know the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the changes in the patient records or the information the voices are giving you are meant for you specifically, or could they be relevant to someone else as well?\n\nPatient: I feel like I'm repeating myself here, but yes, it is meant for me specifically. Like I said, I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern management doesn't want anyone to see. They keep telling me which patient records have been changed. \n\nIt's... it sounds as clear as somebody standing right next to me talking. But the thing is, the unit will be completely quiet, and nobody else hears it. Just me. So, that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to do something or take a specific action?\n\nPatient: No, they don't tell me to do anything. Like I said, it's just voices coming from the call-light speaker and the vents in the hallway. They just keep telling me which patient records have been changed. It's so clear, just like someone standing right there, but nobody else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices constant, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really... timed it or paid attention to a specific pattern. \n\nI guess they kind of come and go. I just know I hear them when I'm out on the floor at work, walking by the vents or near the call-light system. But I couldn't tell you if it's happening every single minute of my shift. I just know they're there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are trying to tell you something that you already know, or something that you couldn't possibly know?\n\nPatient: Well, they're definitely telling me things I already know. Like, I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern that management doesn't want anyone to see. The voices just kind of confirm it. \n\nEven when my supervisor tries to tell me it's just late entries or, you know, coding mistakes... I know better. I can tell they're watching what I write down now because I'm the one who caught the pattern in the first place. The voices are just letting me know exactly which records they're messing with.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are real or could they be a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I mean... they're real. I hear them just as clearly as I hear you talking to me right now. I don't see how it could just be in my head when the information they're giving me matches up with what's actually happening with the charts. It's real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anyone or anything in particular behind the voices or the changes in the patient records?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said before, I know it's management. They're the ones behind it. I saw the pattern in the wound-care charts, and they definitely don't want anyone to see it. \n\nMy supervisor tries to play it off. She tells me it's just late entries or coding mistakes, but... I know better. They know I caught the pattern. That's why I can tell they're watching what I write down now. They're trying to cover it up, and the voices are just helping me keep track of what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think management is specifically targeting you with these changes in patient records?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, like I just said, I'm the one who caught it. I know those wound-care charts are showing a pattern that management doesn't want anyone to see. And even when my supervisor tries to brush it off and says it's just late entries or coding mistakes... I can tell they're watching exactly what I write down now. They are doing it specifically because I'm the one who figured it out. So, yes, they are definitely focused on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think management is actually capable of changing the patient records in the way the voices are telling you they are?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. They have full access to the system. I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern that management doesn't want anyone to see. They have the power to change those records, and they're doing it to cover their tracks.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like management is trying to harm you personally, beyond just covering up the pattern in the patient records?\n\nPatient: I don't know about physical harm, if that's what you mean. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because... well, I keep hearing voices at work. I hear them coming from the call-light speaker, and the vents out in the hallway. They keep telling me which patient records have been changed. \n\nIt's... it sounds as clear as somebody standing right next to me talking. But the thing is, the unit will be completely quiet, and nobody else hears it. Just me. So, that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to do something or take a specific action?\n\nPatient: No, they don't tell me to do anything. Like I said, it's just voices coming from the call-light speaker and the vents in the hallway. They just keep telling me which patient records have been changed. It's so clear, just like someone standing right there, but nobody else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices constant, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really... timed it or paid attention to a specific pattern. \n\nI guess they kind of come and go. I just know I hear them when I'm out on the floor at work, walking by the vents or near the call-light system. But I couldn't tell you if it's happening every single minute of my shift. I just know they're there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are trying to tell you something that you already know, or something that you couldn't possibly know?\n\nPatient: Well, they're definitely telling me things I already know. Like, I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern that management doesn't want anyone to see. The voices just kind of confirm it. \n\nEven when my supervisor tries to tell me it's just late entries or, you know, coding mistakes... I know better. I can tell they're watching what I write down now because I'm the one who caught the pattern in the first place. The voices are just letting me know exactly which records they're messing with.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are real or could they be a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I mean... they're real. I hear them just as clearly as I hear you talking to me right now. I don't see how it could just be in my head when the information they're giving me matches up with what's actually happening with the charts. It's real.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anyone or anything in particular behind the voices or the changes in the patient records?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said before, I know it's management. They're the ones behind it. I saw the pattern in the wound-care charts, and they definitely don't want anyone to see it. \n\nMy supervisor tries to play it off. She tells me it's just late entries or coding mistakes, but... I know better. They know I caught the pattern. That's why I can tell they're watching what I write down now. They're trying to cover it up, and the voices are just helping me keep track of what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think management is specifically targeting you with these changes in patient records?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, like I just said, I'm the one who caught it. I know those wound-care charts are showing a pattern that management doesn't want anyone to see. And even when my supervisor tries to brush it off and says it's just late entries or coding mistakes... I can tell they're watching exactly what I write down now. They are doing it specifically because I'm the one who figured it out. So, yes, they are definitely focused on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think management is actually capable of changing the patient records in the way the voices are telling you they are?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. They have full access to the system. I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern that management doesn't want anyone to see. My supervisor keeps telling me it's just late entries or coding mistakes, but I know what I'm looking at. I can tell they're watching what I write down now because I'm the one who caught it. They have the power to change those records, and they're doing it to cover their tracks.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like management is trying to harm you personally, beyond just covering up the pattern in the patient records?\n\nPatient: I don't know about physical harm, if that's what you mean. But... they definitely want to get rid of me or ruin my job. Like I've been saying, I know those wound-care charts have a pattern they really don't want anyone to see. So, it involves the patients, sure, but the focus is on me because I know the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the changes in the patient records or the information the voices are giving you are meant for you specifically, or could they be relevant to someone else as well?\n\nPatient: I feel like I'm repeating myself here, but yes, it is meant for me specifically. Like I said, I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern management doesn't want anyone to see. And even when my supervisor says it's just late entries or coding mistakes, I can tell they're watching what I write because I'm the one who caught it. The information is coming to me because I'm the one who actually sees what they are doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or upset when you hear the voices or think about management changing the patient records?\n\nPatient: I don't... I wouldn't say I feel anxious. Or upset, really. I mean, I haven't really thought about it like that. It's just what is happening. I guess I'm just focused on it, because I know I'm right about the charts. So, no, not anxious... I'm just paying attention to what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices or the situation with the patient records affected your ability to work or your daily life in any significant way?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, it has. I was actually taken off the floor right in the middle of a shift recently. My supervisor told me that my handoffs and my charting were no longer safe. And at home... my husband has had to step in and manage things that I usually handle. \n\nIt just feels like my reputation at work is ruined, and my plans to go to LPN school are just falling apart because of all this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people at your workplace, besides you, believe that management is changing patient records to cover something up?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think anyone else sees it. Or if they do, they're not saying anything. Like I said, my supervisor just brushes it off as coding mistakes or late entries. Everyone else just goes along with it, just doing their shifts and going home. I'm the only one who really sees the pattern in the wound-care charts. So no, I don't think anyone else believes it. It's just me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that management is capable of using the call-light speaker and vents to communicate with you directly?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do believe they're capable of that. I mean... I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern that management doesn't want anyone to see. My supervisor keeps saying it's just late entries or coding mistakes, but I can tell they're watching exactly what I write. They know I'm the one who caught it. So... using the call-lights and vents to get to me? Yes, absolutely. It's just their way of keeping track of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have a special role or mission to expose the truth about the patient records?\n\nPatient: No... no, I wouldn't call it a special mission or anything like that. And even when my supervisor tries to brush it off and says it's just late entries or coding mistakes... I can tell they're watching exactly what I write down now. They are doing it specifically because I'm the one who figured it out. So, yes, they are definitely focused on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think management is actually capable of changing the patient records in the way the voices are telling you they are?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. They have full access to the system. I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern that management doesn't want anyone to see. They have the power to change those records, and they're doing it to cover their tracks.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like management is trying to harm you personally, beyond just covering up the pattern in the patient records?\n\nPatient: I don't know about physical harm, if that's what you mean.
